**Book 159:** *Buku Panduan Guru Matematika untuk SD/MI Kelas I* (teacher guide) — Indonesia, Dara Retno Wulan and Rasfaniwaty, Kementerian Pendidikan, Kebudayaan, Riset, dan Teknologi — Pusat Perbukuan (Kurikulum Merdeka), 2022, ISBN 978-602-244-875-4. Filed in maths/books/indonesia.md (FULLY READ + CITATION CHECK PASSED). Teacher evidence only: the matched companion guide of the Grade 1 student book cited in this lesson — Panduan Umum (Fase A outcomes and ATP, printed pp. 1–14) plus one guide section per student chapter, with objectives, lesson flow and timing, activities, misconception notes, remedial and enrichment tasks, project rubrics, answer keys keyed to named student-book pages, and reproducible ten-frame, number-bond and game templates (printed pp. 1–200). It teaches no pupil content beyond the student book. The guide reproduces the student book's chapter covers, placeholder ISBN and all; its own ISBN is as given here. Guide citation stocked by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep.

**Book 160:** *Buku Panduan Guru Matematika untuk SD/MI Kelas II* (teacher guide) — Indonesia, Dara Retno Wulan and Rasfaniwaty, Kementerian Pendidikan, Kebudayaan, Riset, dan Teknologi — Pusat Perbukuan (Kurikulum Merdeka), 2022, ISBN 978-602-427-898-4. Filed in maths/books/indonesia.md (FULLY READ + CITATION CHECK PASSED). Teacher evidence only: the matched companion guide of the Grade 2 student book cited in this lesson — general guidance plus one guide section per student chapter, with lesson flow, the two-step word-problem method (known → asked → solution → conclusion) and four keyword cards, misconception handling, parent interaction, assessment rubrics, remedial and enrichment tasks, answer keys and reproducible aids (printed pp. 1–184). It teaches no pupil content beyond the student book. Its Chapter 1 pages (printed pp. 20–42) and Chapter 2 cover (p. 43) misprint the running footer as "Kelas I"; cite the cover and title page. Guide citation stocked by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep.

**Book 161:** *Buku Panduan Guru Matematika untuk SD/MI Kelas III* (teacher guide) — Indonesia, Susanto, Arika Indah Kristiana, Arif Fatahillah, Eko Waluyo, Ridho Alfarisi, Hobri, Kementerian Pendidikan, Kebudayaan, Riset, dan Teknologi — Pusat Perbukuan (Kurikulum Merdeka), 2022, ISBN 978-602-427-936-3. Filed in maths/books/indonesia.md (FULLY READ + CITATION CHECK PASSED). It teaches no pupil content beyond the student book. Guide citation stocked by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep.

**Book 162:** *Buku Panduan Guru Matematika untuk SD/MI Kelas IV* (teacher guide) — Indonesia, Hobri, Susanto, Arika Indah Kristiana, Arif Fatahillah, Eko Waluyo, Ridho Alfarisi, Haris Setiya Budi, Moh. Iqbal Helmi, Kementerian Pendidikan, Kebudayaan, Riset, dan Teknologi — Pusat Perbukuan (Kurikulum Merdeka), 2022, ISBN 978-602-244-909-6. Filed in maths/books/indonesia.md (FULLY READ + CITATION CHECK PASSED). Teacher evidence only: the matched companion guide of the Grade 4 student book cited in this lesson — general guidance (including the Fase B ATP table with per-topic hour allocations, printed pp. 6–17) plus one guide section per student chapter with objectives, teaching guidance and answer keys (printed pp. 1–252). It teaches no pupil content beyond the student book. Its Bab 1 objectives list prints items 1.1–1.7 only; items 1.8–1.10 (operations and factors/multiples) stand in its ATP table and chapter scheme — an objectives-list omission, not missing teaching. Guide citation stocked by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep.

**Book 163:** *Buku Panduan Guru Matematika untuk SD/MI Kelas V* (teacher guide) — Indonesia, Meita Fitrianawati, Ika Surtiani, Ait Istiandaru, Kementerian Pendidikan, Kebudayaan, Riset, dan Teknologi — Pusat Perbukuan (Kurikulum Merdeka), 2022, ISBN 978-602-427-899-1. It teaches no pupil content beyond the student book. Guide citation stocked by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep.

**Acceptance count: taught in 51 countries (Libya, Greece, Sierra Leone, Ghana, Kazakhstan, South Africa, Laos, Yemen, Indonesia, Nicaragua, Ethiopia, South Sudan, Iraq, Bangladesh, Nepal, Cambodia, Kuwait, Egypt, North Macedonia, Iran, China, Pakistan, Syria, Saudi Arabia, Rwanda, Sudan, India, Mongolia, Venezuela, Afghanistan, Uzbekistan, Ecuador, Albania, Tajikistan, Palestine, Turkey, Tanzania, Armenia, Jordan, Mauritania, Vietnam, El Salvador, Cyprus, Sri Lanka, Colombia, Angola, Moldova, Brazil, Azerbaijan, Bhutan, Cabo Verde)**.** Libya carries the primary climb across its «الأصدقاء! يحبون الرياضيات» (Maths Friends) series — Grade 1 to 100 (1–10, then to 20, to 40, then to 100), Grade 2 to 1,000, Grade 3 to 10,000, Grade 4 to 100,000, Grade 5 to 1 million and to 10 million, Grade 6 with no new reach, and Grade 7 place value inside Chapter 1 only (ladder established by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep, which corrected this sentence — it previously read as if one book carried the whole climb). Greece carries the whole primary climb in its own series — Grade 1 to 100 (the table's Greece row cites Unit 3: numbers to 20, sums to 10 and coins), Grade 2 taught to 1,000 with four-digit numbers recognised at year end, Grade 3 to 10,000, Grade 4 to 1,000,000, Grade 5 into the billions in place-value exercises (Workbook A, printed p. 15, works with 156,156,156,156) and Grade 6 through powers of 10 (ladder established by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep, which corrected this sentence — it previously summarised Grade 1 Unit 3 as if it were the whole country). Sierra Leone carries the whole primary climb as teacher evidence, since its Primary set is lesson plans, not a pupil book: Primary 1 to 10, then to 100 (in 1s, 2s and 5s, grouping in 10s); Primary 2 to 100, then to 1,000; Primary 3 to 1,000, then to 10,000; Primary 4 counting to 10,000 (place value and operations reach 100,000); Primary 5 to 100,000, with counting forwards and backwards to 1,000,000 in multiples of 10,000 and 100,000; Primary 6 to 10,000,000 (ladder established by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep and stocked rung by rung in the table above). Ghana carries the same climb as teacher evidence (counting 1–50, 1–100, in bundles) — its set holds no pupil textbook at all. Kazakhstan climbs in Grade 1 to 10 and then 11–20, counting by tens (Akpaeva), with the Zhakupova NIS adaptation reaching 20, two-digit numbers and counting by tens to 100; Grade 2 counts to 100 and meets hundreds as a unit; Grade 3 counts within 1,000 and reads thousands to 1,000,000; Grade 4 counts into the millions class (ladder established by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep, stocked section by section in the table above). South Africa carries counting checked against real things on beads in Grade 1, and the whole climb to 1,000,000 across the set: Grade 1 secures numbers to 10 with patterns in 2s and 5s to 20, then numbers 11–20 with patterns in 5s and 2s to 100 and tens to 50; Grade 2 counts to 50, then to 200; Grade 3 targets 500, then 1,000; Grade 4 climbs 1,000, 2,000, 5,000, 10,000; Grade 5 climbs 10,000, 20,000, 50,000, 100,000; Grade 6 climbs 100,000, 200,000, nine-digit numbers and 1,000,000, and Grade 7 revision works nine-digit numbers (climb proved from the books and itemised worksheet by worksheet in the 2026-11-09 sweep). Other read countries teach counting inside their own Grade 1 books and join by merge.** Laos carries the primary climb across its two series — 1–10 and 0, teens as 10 + n, then counting in tens to 100 with place value in Grade 1, and hundreds in Grade 2 (new series); the old-series Grade 3 book climbs to 1,000, then 10,000, then 100,000, the new-series Grade 4 book goes beyond 10,000, and Grade 5 works beyond 1,000,000 (the upper rungs are stocked in full in the place-value lesson; the Grade 3 and Grade 4 counting rows here were completed by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep). Yemen carries the whole primary climb in one series — 0–10 with zero as the empty set, to 20, to 100, to 1000 in Grade 2, to 10,000 in Grade 3, to 999,999 in Grade 4, to hundreds of millions in Grade 5 and billions in Grade 6. Indonesia carries the whole primary climb in one series — counting to 10 and to 20 in Grade 1, to 50 and to 100 in Grade 2, whole numbers to 1,000 in Grade 3 and to 10,000 in Grade 4, then to 100,000 and to 1,000,000 in Grade 5 (reading and writing only at 1,000,000; operations are not extended there). Nicaragua carries the whole primary climb in two parallel series — numbers to 100 in Grade 1, to 1,000 in Grade 2 (ordinals to 20° in the old book only), to 10,000 in Grade 3, and beyond 10,000 in Grade 4. Ethiopia carries the whole primary climb in one bureau's series — counting to 20 in Grade 1, to 100 in Grade 1, to 1000 in Grade 2, to 10,000 in Grade 3 with Ethiopian (Ge'ez) numerals 1–10, and in Grade 4 to 1,000,000 and past it — §1.7 large numbers reach billions, the place-value chart running to hundred-billions — with Ethiopian counting numerals to 100. South Sudan carries the whole primary climb in one series — counting 1–99 and in tens to 100 in Grade 1, 3-digit numbers in Grade 2, 4-digit numbers in Grade 3, 5-digit numbers in Grade 4, six-digit numbers in Grade 5 and numbers to a million in Grade 6, with natural numbers and place value again in Secondary 1. Iraq carries the whole primary climb in one series — numbers to 20 and to 99 in Grade 1, to 999 in Grade 2, to 9999 in Grade 3, to 9,999,999 in Grade 4, and within billions in Grade 5. Bangladesh carries the whole primary climb in one series — counting and numbers 1–10 with zero in Class 1, to 100 in Class 1 and to 1000 in Class 2, to 10,000 with Ayut and lakh in Class 3, to crore in Class 4, and tally marks, Roman numbers and the number line in Class 6. Nepal carries the whole primary climb in one series — counting 1–5, 6–10 and 11–20 to the full 1–100 table in Grade 1 (teach the p. 51 chart corrected: the last cell of the 21–30 row is 30, not 20), to 1000 in Grade 2, four- and five-digit numbers and lakh to 9,99,999 in Grade 3, to one crore in Grade 4 and to 10 crore in Grade 5. Cambodia carries the whole primary climb in one series — 1–10 and 0 in Grade 1, to 100 in Grade 1, to 1000 in Grade 2, to 10,000 in Grade 3, to 100,000 in Grade 4, to 1,000,000 and beyond in Grade 5, and to 10 digits in Grade 6. Kuwait carries the whole early climb in its own series — numbers 1–5 and on to 12 in Grade 1 Term 1, to 100 in Grade 1 Term 2, to 100 again with the hundred chart in Grade 2 Term 1, by hundreds to 1000 in Grade 2 Term 2, and to 6-digit numbers in Grade 3 Term 1, into the millions in Grade 4 Term 1 and into the billions in Grade 5 Term 1 (those two steps are stocked in the place-value lesson). Egypt carries counting from the start of Primary 1 in its Japanese-collaboration series — counting and matching quantities, the numbers 1–5 and 6–10, and numbers greater than 20 into the thirties and forties in Term 1; counting in tens to 100 and numbers bigger than 100 (to about 125) in Term 2 — and in Primary 2 Term 1 of the same series it builds hundreds and 3-digit numbers, zero in a place (204), and one thousand as ten 100s; its Grade 2 Term 2 old Discover book adds even and odd numbers — deciding by pairing pictured objects, and classifying sums as even or odd (the Primary 1–2 rows were stocked from the in-set books by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep). North Macedonia enters counting at Grade 1 (2022) — counting to 10 and back, the numerals 0–10 one by one, counting to 20 and to 100, by 2s and by 10s, with even and odd numbers. Iran carries the start of the climb in its primary series — counting with objects, tally marks and fingers in Grade 1, numbers built up section by section towards 100, numbers to 999 (three-digit numbers) in Grade 2, and number values in Grade 3. Jordan carries counting from numbers to 10, the number chart and counting up and down in Grade 1, through counting by ones, tens and hundreds in Grade 2, with skip counting in Grade 3 (stocked by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep).

Vietnam carries the whole primary counting ladder in its Kết nối tri thức books — the numbers 0–10 and the 1–100 table in Grade 1, numbers to 1,000 in Grade 2, numbers to 100,000 in Grade 3, and numbers to 1,000,000 with the millions class in Grade 4. Stocked 2026-11-11 by the Vietnam re-verification sweep merge.

Mauritania carries the whole primary climb in its IPN Arabic pupil books — Grade 1 numbers studied one by one to 20 (0 included, tens-and-ones language from 11–20), with the teacher guide’s term-3 progression teaching the bands 21–30, 31–40 and 41–50; Grade 2 to 99 in the pupil book (the guide’s programme runs ahead to 150 — guide-only evidence); Grade 3 to 999; Grade 4 to 9,999, in hundreds bands; Grade 5 to 999,999; Grade 6 large natural numbers with no named ceiling; and 1AS (Grade 7) Entiers naturels reaching millions and milliards (this ladder was established by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep, which found this lesson stocking no Mauritania row at all).

China carries counting in the primary Volume 2 books — to 100 in Grade 1, built on the hundred chart, and to 10,000 in Grade 2.

Pakistan (Punjab) carries counting from the nursery primer upward — numbers to 50 in the Primer (its ordinal numbers stand on their own page, printed p. 138, stocked in the table above by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep merge), to 100 in Grade 1 and to 1,000 in Grade 2 — and returns to counting at Class 11, where the PECTAA mathematics book builds the fundamental counting principle, factorials, permutations and combinations.

Syria carries the primary climb in semester books — numbers to 9 and zero as the empty set in Grade 1 Semester 1, then the number 10 as a full ten-frame and the numbers 11 and 12 in the same book (Chapter 4, printed pp. 33, 40 — stocked by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep merge, which found both rungs cited nowhere); teens as ten plus ones, counting by tens to 100 and skip-counting in twos and fives in Semester 2; the number 100 and counting by hundreds in Grade 2; counting in thousands to 10,000 in Grade 3 — and returns to counting in Grade 6 and Grade 7, where the natural numbers are defined as the set {0, 1, 2, …} on the number line, and in Grade 9 Algebra, where π enters historically as circumference ÷ diameter.

Saudi Arabia counts with the same one-number-at-a-time build in Grade 1 — count, name, model with counters, then read and write — and reaches skip counting by twos, fives and tens on the hundred chart in Part Two, with Grade 2 Part One colouring skip-count patterns on the chart. Kuwait gains further practice evidence here from a teacher-made Grade 3 booklet misfiled in Saudi Arabia's record: sequence-completion and before/after/between exercises (PDF pp. 14–20), cited as a Kuwaiti school booklet, not as a textbook.

Rwanda teaches counting as a staged climb in its REB primary books — to 200, 500 and 1,000 in the Year 2 pupil's book (in both the English edition and the Kinyarwanda original), on to 2,000, 5,000 and 10,000 in Year 3, reading and writing numbers to 100,000 in P4 (with forming numerals from given digits), to 1,000,000 in P5 and beyond 1,000,000 in P6, and even, odd, prime and square numbers revisited as subsets of the natural numbers in Senior 1.

Sudan counts one number at a time in Grade 1 — each number 1 to 9 counted, traced, written and matched to its sets, zero taught last of the ten — then the number before and the number after, and counting in tens.

India carries counting through its NCERT ladder — Math-Magic Classes 1–4 and Mathematics Class 6 — from one-to-one matching and counting rhymes in Class 1, through counting in groups and counting in tens in Class 2, to one lakh (one hundred thousand) named in Class 4.


Mongolia enters through its ministry textbooks Математик I, II, V (Grades 1, 2, 5): counting built one number at a time — each number 1 to 10 with its split-and-join bonds, zero from the emptying leaf, ordinals from a race podium — then counting in tens to 100 and in ones, tens and hundreds to 1000.

Venezuela enters through the Colección Bicentenario, the MPPE's one-book-per-grade course: *Contemos... 1, 2, 3 y 4* (Grade 1), *Triángulos, rectángulos y algo más...* (Grade 2) and *Conciencia Matemática* (Grade 8). From its books: from Grade 1: counting 1 to 5 — counting song: children arrive one by one up to five; showing 1–5 on fingers; numbers UNO to CINCO in words; from Grade 2: sequences in tables — activity: repeated-addition sequences (×5 reading) stated as sequences like the multiplication tables; from Grade 8: recreational — reverse-and-add palindrome (capicúa) process: 9.253 + 3.529 = 12.782 → … → 583.385, a palindrome.

Afghanistan enters through its Ministry of Education course — the Grade 1 course in two language editions (Pashto and Dari, one work with identical page maps) and Dari student books for Grades 4, 6, 7, 8, 10, 11 and 12, all printing year 1398 HS except the Grade 8 book, which prints 1399 HS on its cover and title page and 1398 HS on its specifications page. From its books: from Grade 1: number 1: recognise, count and write (Lesson 1); number 1 practice and homework; number 2: recognise, read and write (Lesson 2); number 2 practice and homework; number 3: recognise, count, read and write (Lesson 3); counting practice 1-3: write the count, match the numeral; counting objects 1-3 by matching pairs; number 4: recognise, count, read and write (Lesson 4); number 4 practice: count and write; numbers 1-4 practice: circle the correct numeral; number 5: recognise, count, read and write (Lesson 5); number 5 practice; number sequence 1-5 with gaps to fill; number 6: recognise, count, read and write (Lesson 8); numbers 7 and 8: recognise, count, read and write (Lesson 9); number 9: recognise, count, read and write (Lesson 10); zero: recognise the number 0 (Lesson 11); counting practice including empty sets (zero); counting bean pictures and circling the count (numbers to 9); the number 10: counting sets of ten (Lesson 20); counting two-digit numbers to 20: count and write the total; writing numbers to 99 in order: the 1-99 grid (Lesson 34); counting in 2s and in 3s, forwards and backwards; the spiral number track to 99; counting in 2s and in 3s; matching numerals to number words to 99; from Grade 7: natural (counting) numbers introduced.

Albania enters through its Mediaprint teacher's books (Libër Mësuesi, in Albanian) — TEACHER EVIDENCE only: no pupil's book is in hand for any grade, so Albania is never cited as a pupil-textbook source; what the teacher's books evidence is what is taught and in what order, through their lesson plans, worked answers and answer keys. From its books: from Grade 1 (teacher's book): Overview: counting, number and operations together; zero as first number; number lines/tracks, counting…; Chapter opener, Diskuto: say numbers in order 1–100, count back 20–0; numbers in pictures/everyday life…; Counting objects: count objects to 20, find the quantity of a number; hidden-object counting, totals stay the…; Counting while playing: count objects to 20 through rhymes, finger numbers and the fishing game; number bonds…; I now know: revision — count in order, count objects to 20, read/write 0–20; board game counting on from 9 to…; Test yourself: count 1–100 forwards and 20–0 back, 100-square, make two-digit numbers from digit cards 0–9…; Number lines: find 2 more or 2 less than a number to 20 recording steps on a number line; 8 drum beats and 2…; First Zbulo sheet, mislabelled — see band draft: games applying 1/10 more or less and estimating (pupil book….

Tajikistan enters through its Ministry-approved pupil's textbooks in Tajik (Cyrillic), all published in Dushanbe — Математика for Grade 5 (Маориф, 2022) and Grade 6 (5th edition, Маориф, 2021), Алгебра for Grades 7 (4th improved edition, 2023) and 8 (4th edition, 2023), and Геометрия — the continuation of stereometry — for Grade 11 (3rd edition, Лавҳ, 2020). From its books: from Grade 5: Natural numbers and zero.

Palestine enters through the pupil's books of its Ministry of Education (Curriculum Centre, Ramallah), all in Arabic — Mathematics Grade 1 in two parts (both fourth edition, 2020), Grade 10 in two parts (Part One third edition, 2020; Part Two second edition, 2019 — the held parts are different editions and are cited as such), and the Grade 12 Technological-stream book (التكنولوجي, first edition, 2019 — the book's own name for its stream, never "Information Technology"). No ISBN is printed in any of the five books. From its books: from Grade 1 Part One: one-to-one correspondence (stone per sheep) as the reason for counting; the number 1: recognise, read, chant/clap, match numeral to set, colour 1, trace/write digit ١; the number 2: recognise, read, match numeral to set, colour 2, trace/write digit ٢; the number 3: recognise, read, match numeral to set, colour 3, trace/write digit ٣; count a pictured set and write its number (1–3 consolidation); the number 4: recognise, read, match numeral to set, trace/write digit ٤; the number 5: recognise, read, match numeral to set, trace/write digit ٥; the number 6: recognise, read, count and write, trace/write digit ٦; split a set into two groups by given numbers (part–whole within 6); the number 7: recognise, read, trace/write digit ٧; the number 8: recognise, read, trace/write digit ٨; the number 9: recognise, read, trace/write digit ٩; complete a drawing up to a written number 1–9; write the numbers 1–9 and count; review: missing number in a 1–9 sequence; split sets into two groups; the number zero: count of an empty set (empty envelope, empty cage), write ٠; the number 10: grouping of ten, count to 10; 10 on the number line; count backward from 10; components (bonds) of 10; numbers 11–19 in figures and words, as ten + ones; the number 20 as 2 tens; count on past 19 to 20; review: match numbers to word names (fifteen, nineteen…), count and compare pictured sets to 20; from Grade 1 Part Two: numbers 21–29 in figures and words (twenty-two … twenty-nine); represent numbers: colour heard numbers in a number grid; numbers on the abacus; train-stop numbers; numbers 30–99: read and write the full two-digit range; write numbers heard in sentences (seventy pupils; 96 sheep); continue count patterns into the 90s; multiples of ten: build complete tens from sticks and count 10, 20, …, 90; multiples of ten on the number line (40, 70, 80); ten sequences forward and back; unit review: write numbers from words (eighty-nine; 40 + 6); skip-count by twos (frog jumps).

Barbados enters as CURRICULUM EVIDENCE only, through the *Primary Mathematics Syllabus* (Classes 1–4) of its Ministry of Education and Human Resource Development — its Class 1 syllabus requires reading and writing numbers up to 999; Class 2 extends the requirement to 9,999 (the Class 2 requirement's row is filed in the place-value lesson); Class 3 extends the requirement to 99,999 and Class 4 to 9,999,999. A syllabus states what is to be taught, in what order and to what depth; it teaches nothing itself. Barbados publishes no government pupil textbooks — primary mathematics books are commercial titles parents buy, and the syllabi's Suggested Texts appendices name them (*Caribbean Primary Mathematics* Levels 1–6; *Nelson Primary Maths for Caribbean Schools* 1–4; *Steps To Common Entrance Mathematics* 1–3) as named by the syllabus only: none is in hand and none is cited. Barbados is therefore named in this lesson's countries line and its syllabus requirements are cited in the table as curriculum evidence, but Barbados is not counted in the taught-in figure.

Turkey enters through the pupil's textbooks of its Millî Eğitim Bakanlığı (MEB — Ministry of National Education), all in Turkish, first volumes (1. Kitap) only — Grade 3 (ISBN 978-975-11-9897-6, TTKB approval 13/07/2026), Grade 6 (ISBN 978-975-11-8773-4, TTKB approval 30.06.2025) and Grade 7 (ISBN 978-975-11-9899-0, TTKB approval 1.07.2026); no edition, printing or year line is printed in any of the three books, and the grades' second volumes (2. Kitap) are not in hand and are cited nowhere. From its books: from Grade 3: read and write three-digit numbers to 1000: pictured sets counted and matched to their word forms (99, 100, 108, 223); read and write three-digit numbers including zero tens/ones (113, 240, 308, 476, 760, 933) in figures and Turkish words; three-digit numbers in daily life: find, write and read aloud numbers from real contexts (page counts, the 112 emergency number, city phone codes, own height in cm); read numbers written on signs, shirts and placards in a fairground picture and say how each is read; rhythmic (skip) counting recall: complete forward sequences in 2s, 3s, 4s and 5s; the 4th number said after 20 counting in 4s; a rabbit hopping 2s on a number line; skip counting as daily saving: two money boxes filled 5 TL and 6 TL a day tabulated over 10 days; forward skip counting in 6s from pictured multiples: 6 tulips per bouquet, totals for 1–10 bouquets (6, 12, 18 … 60); forward skip counting in 7s: 7 strawberries per plate, totals for 1–10 plates; forward skip counting in 8s: 8 carrots per plate, totals for 1–10 plates; forward skip counting in 9s: 9 leaves per flower, totals for 1–10 flowers; forward skip counting in 10s: 10 eggs per tray, totals for 1–10 trays; forward skip counting in 100s: 100 pages per notebook, totals for 1–10 notebooks (100 … 1000); what stays the same between the numbers said; backward skip counting in 6s as stock runs out: 60 pencils selling one 6-pack a day (60, 54, 48 …); complete backward runs in 7s and 9s drawn as falling stairs (70, 63 …; 90, 81 …); backward skip counting in 10s: 100 beads selling one 10-bead bracelet a day (100, 90, 80 …); complete backward runs in 100s, 8s and 6s; sticky-note race game: write the 6s, 7s, 8s, 9s and 10s sequences on notes, scramble them on the board, rebuild each forward sequence fastest; then play backward too; human number line: pupils holding the 6s sequence cards line up at equal spacings along a rope in the right order; repeat for 7s–10s, forward and backward; fill the missing terms of forward sequences in 7s, 9s, 8s, 6s, 10s and 100s; design a picture that contains a 6-to-60 skip count; research rhythmic counting from trusted (gov.tr / edu.tr) sources and check information before sharing it; classify lettered sequences a–g as forward/backward and by step (6s, 7s, 8s, 9s), one letter can match several descriptions; performance task: build a wipe-clean hundred chart from recycled card and colour in the 6s, 7s, 8s, 9s, 10s and 100s counts, forward and backward; from Grade 7: Integers first motivated by contexts where zero has no natural origin (temperature scale, timeline) vs contexts with a natural zero (mass, length).

Tanzania enters through the pupil's book of its Tanzania Institute of Education (TIE) — *Mathematics Standard Five — Pupil's Book*, the English-medium Standard Five book (© TIE 2019, published 2019, reprinted 2021; ISBN 978-9987-09-109-6; Certificate of Approval No. 282, approved by the Ministry of Education, Science and Technology on 5 December 2019, prepared to the 2016 primary Mathematics Syllabus). It is the only Tanzania mathematics book in hand: the other 20 recorded works — among them the Swahili-medium Hisabati Std 5, a separate book from this English one — are waiting-for-file and are cited nowhere. From its book: from Standard Five: whole numbers defined as counting numbers with zero, with no fractional or decimal parts and no negatives; number patterns and sequences revised to 99,999; counting in groups to 500,000 (ten 10,000-cards make 100,000); counting to 1,000,000 (ten 100,000-packages make one million); reading and writing numbers up to 1,000,000 in numerals and in words.

Armenia enters through its Mathematics 1, Part 1 (student textbook-notebook, 2025); Mathematics 3, Part 1 (student); the Mathematics 1 methodological guide (teacher evidence); Mathematics 5 (student). From its books: counting objects: the numbers 1–5 (counting starts from one); counting objects: the numbers 6–10; the digit (թվանշան) as a sign for a number; writing the number one with a digit; writing the number two with a digit; writing the number three with a digit; writing the number four with a digit; writing the number five with a digit; writing the number six with a digit; writing the number seven with a digit; composition of a number (what a number is made of); writing the number eight with a digit; composition of the number eight; writing the number nine with a digit; composition of the number nine; writing the number zero with a digit; four-digit numbers: forming, naming, writing 1000–9999; five-digit and six-digit numbers; writing the number "ten" in digits; ten as a counting unit; position of numbers up to 10; numbers of the second ten — counting, reading and writing numbers 11–20; two-digit round tens — naming and writing round tens to 100 (10, 20, 30, 40, …); adding and subtracting round tens; measuring a segment's length in centimetres; numbering of buildings (number order on a street; odd/even sides); the natural-number sequence; reading numbers.

## Morocco added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Morocco's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **32 books, all of them teacher guides or teacher editions (Dar Attakafa, Casablanca); 0 pupil (student) books** — and placed 610 teaching items. No Moroccan pupil maths textbook is in hand: the pupil's books are commercial print, and the library's systematic free shelf is Dar Attakafa's teacher-guide library. Every Morocco item in this section is therefore TEACHER EVIDENCE — a guide states the pupil book's lesson, its content and its order — cited as the evidence kind it is; Morocco is counted in NO lesson's taught-in figure, on the standing Ghana evidence-only precedent, until Moroccan pupil books are in hand and read. Set reconciliation, as the placements prove it: the 32 are distinct works (Le Monde des Maths ×6, L'Explicite en Maths ×6, Al Moufid primary ×3, Al Marjie primary ×2, Al Moufid collège ×3, Switch On! guides ×6, Switch On! Every Day booklets ×6; French 27 books, Arabic 5); textbooks.tsv carries only 28 of the 32 — a library-record gap, reported here and not edited (merges never edit the records). The primary guides all teach the one official revised programme (July 2020) — 32 lessons per grade, Grades 1–6, stated independently by up to four series per grade; the collège guides give the BIOF programme distributions for Grades 7–9. Read depth, as the placements declare it: the Arabic guides' legacy font encoding makes pdftotext return mojibake, so their contents were read from rendered pages; Books 6, 7 and 25 place their own year's programme distribution only (their readings of the other collège years are excluded, so nothing is double-counted); Book 31 places the distinct rows of its yearly programme table, a coarser grain than the lesson-by-lesson guides. The placements' counting grain is one item per distinct lesson/topic the guide teaches the teacher to deliver, and its named exclusions — method/framework chapters, evaluation/bilan/support apparatus and the Switch On confirmation tests, Switch On 1's graphisme weeks, bibliographies, indexes and recording tables — are stocked nowhere. Into this lesson the placements stock 51 teacher-evidence items from 19 of the guides (Book 1, Book 2, Book 3, Book 4, Book 5, Book 6, Book 8, Book 10, Book 19, Book 20, Book 22, Book 23, Book 24, Book 26, Book 27, Book 28, Book 29, Book 30, Book 31; Grades 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7). The number reach the guides evidence runs: counting to 99 (Grade 1) → 999 and 9,999 (Grades 2–3) → 999,999 (Grade 4) → millions and billions (Grades 5–6).

**Book 1:** *Le Monde des Maths 1, Guide de l'enseignant(e), 1re année primaire* (teacher guide, Grade 1, French) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 1,775,505 bytes, 201 PDF pages. The collection follows the revised curriculum/programmes of July 2020 (32 lessons in 6 units over 4 domains); items are the guide's 32 numbered lessons, printed pp. from its Table des matières. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

**Book 2:** *Le Monde des Maths 2, Guide de l'enseignant(e), 2e année primaire* (teacher guide, Grade 2, French) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 9,555,550 bytes. Same collection and July-2020 revised programme as Book 1; items are its 32 numbered lessons — printed pp. from its Table des matières where the sommaire prints them, later lessons cited by sequence (seq.). TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

**Book 3:** *L'Explicite en Maths CP, Guide pédagogique* (CP = Grade 1) (teacher guide, French) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 21,092,403 bytes, 116 PDF pages. The explicit-teaching method guide for the pioneer-schools programme; its Chapter 3 gives worked lesson sheets for the SAME 32-lesson Grade 1 programme as Book 1 — a shared programme, two different guides, cited separately, never merged. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

**Book 4:** *المفيد في الرياضيات (Al Moufid fi al-Riyadiyat), دليل الأستاذة والأستاذ* (teacher guide, Grade 1, Arabic) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 7,559,907 bytes; new edition September 2019, with ministry approval panel. Its contents table lists the same 32-lesson revised programme as Books 1 and 3, in Arabic; the PDF's legacy font encoding makes pdftotext return mojibake, so the contents were read from rendered pages. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

**Book 5:** *L'Explicite en Maths CE1, Guide pédagogique* (CE1 = Grade 2) (teacher guide, French) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 18,580,797 bytes. Items are its 32 lesson sheets, printed pp. from its Sommaire — the Grade 2 programme corresponding to Book 2. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

**Book 6:** *Al Moufid, Guide de l'enseignant(e), 1ère année du cycle secondaire collégial* (1AC = Grade 7, BIOF) (teacher guide, French) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 3,742,780 bytes, 173 PDF pages. Chapters 1–2 are method framework (excluded in the placements); items are the distinct topics of the 1AC programme distribution (printed p. 73). TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

**Book 8:** *Switch On! 1re année, Guide de l'enseignant* (teacher guide, Grade 1, French) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 3,979,650 bytes, 88 PDF pages. A 5-minute daily calculation-drill guide (Japanese method, dir. Prof. Akira Sato); items are the distinct drill strands in its Sommaire (tracks A and B, weeks 1–34). Its graphisme weeks 1–2 (pencil-stroke practice, no mathematics content) and its confirmation tests (assessment apparatus) are excluded in the placements. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

**Book 10:** *المرجع في الرياضيات (Al Marjie fi al-Riyadiyat), دليل الأستاذة والأستاذ* (teacher guide, Grade 3, Arabic) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 18,309,585 bytes, 351 PDF pages. Its contents (read from rendered pages — legacy font encoding) list 32 numbered lessons in 6 units, the Grade 3 programme. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

**Book 19:** *المفيد في الرياضيات (Al Moufid fi al-Riyadiyat), دليل الأستاذة والأستاذ* (teacher guide, Grade 5, Arabic) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 70,552,470 bytes, 330 PDF pages; new edition September 2020, ministry approval serial 515220 (3 Aug 2020). Its contents list 32 numbered lessons in 6 units, read from rendered pages (legacy font encoding). TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

**Book 20:** *L'Explicite en Maths CM2, Guide pédagogique* (CM2 = Grade 5) (teacher guide, French) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 7,455,488 bytes, 122 PDF pages. Items are its 32 lesson sheets, printed pp. from its Sommaire — the Grade 5 programme, corresponding topic-for-topic to Book 19. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

**Book 22:** *Le Monde des Maths 5, Guide de l'enseignant(e), 5e année primaire* (teacher guide, Grade 5, French) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 7,773,818 bytes, 256 PDF pages. Items are its 32 numbered lessons, cited by sequence from its Table des matières. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

**Book 23:** *Le Monde des Maths 6, Guide de l'enseignant(e), 6e année primaire* (teacher guide, Grade 6, French) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 7,986,682 bytes, 300 PDF pages. Items are its 32 numbered lessons, printed pp. from its Table des matières. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

**Book 24:** *L'Explicite en Maths 6e, Guide pédagogique* (Grade 6) (teacher guide, French) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 8,497,260 bytes, 120 PDF pages. **Book 29:** *L'Explicite en Maths CM1, Guide pédagogique* (CM1 = Grade 4) (teacher guide, French) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 8,896,704 bytes. Items are its 32 lesson sheets, printed pp. from its Sommaire — the Grade 4 programme, corresponding to Books 26 and 30. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

## El Salvador added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read El Salvador's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — the MINEDUCYT **ESMATE** programme (Proyecto de Mejoramiento de los Aprendizajes en Matemática en Educación Básica y Educación Media, MINEDUCYT–JICA, 2015–2019): **63 filed entries = 43 distinct works** — 16 student files covering the 11 grade textbooks (Grades 1–11; Grade 1 in 4 part-files, Grade 2 in 3), 25 teacher guides, and 2 JICA materials documents (the materials-use guideline and the Japan–El Salvador unit list) filed 11 times each, byte-identical, MD5-proved — the 2 documents are programme apparatus with no teaching content, cited once each in the country record and stocked in no lesson. The read placed 383 teaching items: 281 from the student textbooks and 102 teacher-evidence items from the guides (one per unit per grade). In the sections below the student books are cited as Books S1–S13 and the guides as Books T1–T12, in grade order. El Salvador COUNTS in this lesson's taught-in figure on the strength of its student-textbook rows; its guide rows are TEACHER EVIDENCE — the guides reproduce the student lessons with teaching plans, worked solutions and a unit test per unit — cited as the evidence kind they are and never the basis of the count. Book errors: none that changes a placement (the Grade 9 book's history opener dates al-Khwārizmī to c. 825 BC; he lived c. 780–850 AD — a front-matter error in an excluded motivation essay, in no teaching item, so nothing is taught from it). The placements' named exclusions — *Recortables* cut-out pages, covers, credits and unit-opener history/motivation essays, the GeoGebra practice lessons of Grades 10–11, end-of-unit and end-of-trimester tests in the student books, teacher-guide front matter, and the Grade 6 book's *Repaso* review sections — are stocked nowhere.

Into this lesson the placements stock 14 items (8 from student books, 6 teacher evidence) from Book S1, Book S2, Book T1, Book T2, Book S3, Book T3, Book S5, Book T4, Book S6, Book T5.

## Cyprus added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Cyprus's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **75 distinct works: 22 pupil textbooks (primary Grades 1–6, part-books) and 53 teacher guides (Οδηγός Εκπαιδευτικού), all Cyprus Ministry of Education (Υπουργείο Παιδείας), in Greek** — and placed 281 teaching items: 228 from the pupil books and 53 teacher-evidence items (one per guide, worded from the guide's stated success indicators, ΔΕΙΚΤΕΣ ΕΠΙΤΥΧΙΑΣ). SET CORRECTION: the expansion map recorded Cyprus Maths as 22 student + 43 teacher = 65 in hand; the records' union proves 22 student + 53 teacher = **75** (staging books.json carries 64 files = 22 student + 42 teacher, all secured in GitHub; textbooks.tsv adds 11 further teacher guides held as git-blob files that are in no books.json: Grade 1 Units 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 7, 9, 10, 11, Grade 2 Unit 1, Grade 4 Unit 1) — the correction is recorded here; the library records themselves are not edited (merges never edit the records). In the sections below the pupil books are cited as Books S1–S22 and the guides as Books T1–T53, in grade order. Cyprus COUNTS in this lesson's taught-in figure on the strength of its pupil-book rows; its guide rows are TEACHER EVIDENCE — a guide states the pupil book's unit, its indicators and its lesson organisation — cited as the evidence kind they are and never the basis of the count (the standing Ghana evidence precedent for guide rows). The read's three flagged claims are carried exactly: (1) the map's “65” is corrected above; (2) the Grade 3 Unit 1 teacher guide's indicator Αρ2.1 states quantities “to 10,000” where its pupil book's Unit 1 teaches to 1,000 — the guide's figure is NOT confirmed by the pupil book and is worded as the guide's own claim in that guide's row, never as pupil teaching; (3) three guides carry the books' own unit-number mislabels — the Grade 4 Unit 3 guide's contents header reads “ΕΝΟΤΗΤΑ 2”, the Grade 6 Unit 3 guide's running head reads “ΕΝΟΤΗΤΑ 2”, the Grade 6 Unit 9 guide's running head reads “ΕΝΟΤΗΤΑ 8” — the contents match their cover units, so the rows record the true units with the errors noted in the book entries, taught corrected, never as printed. Production date stamps inside three files (Grade 5 Part 1 “22/06/2026”, Grade 6 Part 1's InDesign stamp 05/03/2026, Grade 6 Part 3 “01/07/2026”) are PDF production stamps in the ministry files as held, not edition dates. The placements' named exclusions — front matter, icon keys, contents pages, cut-out card appendices (“Για τη σελίδα …”), Διαμορφωτική Αξιολόγηση (formative assessment) pages and enrichment sets repeating unit content — are stocked nowhere; the apparent “330C/20C” in the Grade 3 Part 1 enrichment is the degree sign merging with the digits in the text layer (33 °C falling by 2 °C), not a book error. Teacher-guide coverage in hand: Grades 1–3 Units 1–12 complete, Grade 4 Units 1–3 only, Grade 5 Units 1–4, Grade 6 Units 1–10; the further guides that exist only as ministry hub links (Grade 4 Units 4+, Grade 5 Units 5+, Grade 6 Units 11+) are not in hand, not read and not counted. Cyprus's class for Maths is **COURSES-CLOSED** — every in-hand book (75/75) has been read and placed; if the hub-link guides are later secured, Cyprus reverts to PARTIAL until they are read.

Into this lesson the placements stock 14 items (11 from pupil books, 3 teacher evidence) from Book S1, Book S3, Book S6, Book S10, Book S13, Book T2, Book T3, Book T24.

## Sri Lanka added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Sri Lanka's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs in two passes — **94 files / 66 distinct works: 26 pupil textbooks and 40 teacher guides, all Sri Lanka Educational Publications Department (EPD) / National Institute of Education (NIE), in the country's three media — English, Sinhala and Tamil** — and placed 479 items: 289 from the pupil books and 190 teacher-evidence items (the secondary guides at one item per term of the guide's own lesson sequence; the primary guides at one item per syllabus competency group or per part). Pass 1 read the expansion map's 59 files (32 works); Pass 2 pinned 35 further in-hand files the map's subject filter had missed — 34 NIE teacher guides for Grades 1–13 and one pupil textbook (Book S26, filed under the subject "Science for Technology") — and read every one. In the sections below the pupil books are cited as Books S1–S26 and the guides as Books T1–T40. Sri Lanka COUNTS in a lesson's taught-in figure only on the strength of its pupil-book rows; its guide rows are TEACHER EVIDENCE — a guide states its syllabus's competencies, its lesson sequence and its teaching instructions — cited as the evidence kind they are and never the basis of the count (the standing Ghana evidence precedent for guide rows). Set facts carried exactly: (1) the Grade 9 Sinhala pupil book is held only as chapter files of two different printings (Books S9–S10) whose shared chapter 20 is a different chapter in each, and NO chapter-26 file exists in either split — the English twin's chapter 26 is Algebraic fractions; the hole is carried as a set gap, not as read; (2) the 2018 Sinhala Grade 9 teacher guide is filed twice (two scans of one work) and is placed once (Book T2), and the file held as the Grade 7 2008 manual's "Tamil" copy is byte-identical to the English manual (same git blob SHA) and is cited once, as the English manual (Book T10); (3) the year-less Tamil Grade 4 and Grade 5 guides are proved by byte size to be NIE's 2015 editions, not the far larger 2019/2020 scans NIE also lists (Books T39–T40); (4) the 1976 Grade 12 Mathematics Part I scan (Book S23) ends with some six pages of an unrelated German textbook's verb tables (PDF pp. 271–276) — its mathematics runs to PDF p. 270 and its last chapters were sampled, not contents-verified; (5) the Grade 1 Sinhala 2026 guide is in hand as a Term 1 volume only (Book T33) — its Terms 2–3 are stocked nowhere — and the Grade 1 Tamil 2016 guide (Book T31) is marked DRAFT on its cover and is placed as filed; (6) the Grade 11 guide's Term 2 table labels one lesson (competency 23.6, inside the geometry cluster) "Central limit theorem" — recorded as printed in Book T19's entry; no row in this course rests on that label; (7) Grade 10 Mathematics Part I (English) and Grade 7 Mathematics Part I (Tamil, 2007–2015 edition) are NOT in hand in any filing — library gaps, not unread books; their twins in the other medium are held and read (Books S17 and S13/S14). The placements' named exclusions — revision exercises, answers, glossaries, assessment apparatus, and the guides' specimen lesson plans and school-based assessment instruments beyond their sequences — are stocked nowhere. Sri Lanka's class for Maths is **COURSES-CLOSED** — every in-hand book (94/94 files) has been read and placed, on the in-hand definition.

## Colombia added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Colombia's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **27 distinct works: 26 student books and 1 teacher guide, all Colombia Ministerio de Educación Nacional (MEN), in Spanish** — Escuela Nueva Matemáticas cartillas Grades 1–5 (11), Nivelemos Matemáticas student workbooks Grades 1–4 (4), Aulas sin fronteras Grade 6 Unidad 1 student book and its teacher guide (2), Postprimaria student guides Grades 6–9 (4), Secundaria Activa student modules Grades 6–9 (4), and Vamos a Aprender Matemáticas 10 and 11 (2) — 3,928 PDF pages — and placed 293 teaching items: 288 from the student books and 5 teacher-evidence items from the one teacher guide (Book 23). SET CORRECTIONS carried exactly: (1) the expansion map's “28 student + 1 teacher = 29” over-counts by 2 — textbooks.tsv carries duplicate records of Escuela Nueva Grado 1 Cartilla 1 and Grado 2 Cartilla 1 (old blob/main rows for the same two works that also landed as release assets); the distinct set is 27 works and the duplicates are cited once (the library records themselves are not edited — merges never edit the records); (2) the set in hand contains NO Grade 3 Cartilla 3 — Grade 3 holds Cartillas 1–2 plus the Nivelemos Grade 3 workbook, and Cartilla 2 completes Unidades 6–9; nothing in hand is a mislabelled Cartilla 3, and no never-published ruling is needed or made; (3) Nivelemos Matemáticas Grado 1 (Book 10) has NO asset in any GitHub release — it was read from the MEN source file itself, landed at exactly the census size (5,020,415 bytes) with EOF intact; the library side still owes that file a GitHub landing. The two Vamos a Aprender books (Books 26–27) are image-only viewer assemblies with no text layer, read from rendered pages (covers, printed contents pp. 6–7 and body spot-checks — Matemáticas 10 printed p. 110 “Teorema del seno”, Matemáticas 11 printed p. 120 “Derivada de una función en un punto”); printed page = PDF page in the sampled range. In the sections below the books are cited as Books 1–27 in the placements' order. Colombia COUNTS in a lesson's taught-in figure on the strength of its student-book rows; Book 23's rows are TEACHER EVIDENCE — the guide companions Book 15 with teaching plans, activity scripts and answers and teaches no student content itself — cited as the evidence kind it is and never the basis of the count (the standing Ghana evidence precedent for guide rows). Book errors: none material at placement grain — the Secundaria Activa Grade 9 module's contents page misprints two of its own headings (“Captítulo”, “Undiad 2”); the content is unaffected and rows use the corrected unit names. The placements' named exclusions — presentation letters, “how this book works” pages, author/imprint pages, transversal civics/health/environment themes, practice/evaluation apparatus (“Practica más”, “Evaluación del aprendizaje”, answer keys) and the Escuela Nueva “Estándares/Competencias” boxes at each unidad opening (MEN's own curriculum-standard statements; the taught content is placed at the guías themselves) — are stocked nowhere. The Círculos de Aprendizaje integrated books and the Retos para Gigantes Transición weekly guides are NOT in this set (filed as integrado, not matematicas) and are stocked nowhere. Colombia's class for Maths is **COURSES-CLOSED** — every in-hand Maths book (27/27) has been read and placed, on the in-hand definition.

## Andorra added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Andorra's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **4 documents, all of them official Escola Andorrana curriculum programmes published by decree in the Butlletí Oficial del Principat d'Andorra (BOPA); 0 pupil (student) books** — and placed 679 curriculum-requirement items. A programme states what is REQUIRED to be taught; it teaches nothing itself. Every Andorra item in this section is therefore TEACHER EVIDENCE — a curriculum requirement, worded as a requirement — cited as the evidence kind it is; Andorra is counted in NO lesson's taught-in figure, on the standing Ghana evidence-only precedent, until Andorran pupil books are in hand and read. Set correction, as the placements prove it: the expansion map recorded 3 books in hand; the true set is 4 — the grades 1–6 programme (Decret 376/2021, Annex VII) is held in the GitHub repo but sits in no staging books.json, which is why the map missed it. All four programmes are Andorra's OWN (Govern d'Andorra, in Catalan); the Spanish and French systems also taught in Andorra (Spain's Anaya and Innovamat manuals; France's Le Livre Scolaire) are list-only names on official school book lists — no file is held, so they are cited nowhere in this course and credited to no country — and the Escola Andorrana itself has no commercial pupil-textbook series, so programmes are all that exist to hold. The placements' counting grain is one item per distinct stated requirement — each specific competency, and each distinct entry in the programmes' learning-resource tables, tagged by cycle where the table splits by cycle — and a procedure entry that merely restates a concept entry is folded into it, not double-counted. The placements' named exclusions — the decree preambles, the introductions and competency rationale, the didactic orientations, the assessment criteria and end-of-cycle expectations, the synthesis tables (verified verbatim repeats of the competency sections), the attitudes/values lists, Book 1's glossary and opening summary table, and the Council of Europe cross-references — are stocked nowhere. Book 3's synthesis table words its C2 end-of-cycle expectation as modelling "situacions cientificotècniques" (scientific-technical) inside the social-sciences programme — a copy-over slip in the decree as published; Book 3's competency section itself is consistently social-sciences, and the slip is copied into no row below. Book 2 states its cone/polyhedron sections twice (forms table and models table, printed pp. 32–33) — counted once in the placements and stocked once. Printed page = PDF page in all four books (Books 2–4 carry the BOPA folio; Book 1's Portal PDF is paginated at the foot the same way). Into this lesson the placements stock 13 teacher-evidence items from Book 1, Book 2, Book 4.

**Book 1:** *Programa de matemàtiques de primera ensenyança* (curriculum programme, Grades 1–6) — Andorra, Govern d'Andorra, Escola Andorrana; Decret 376/2021, Annex VII; ISBN 978-99920-0-934-5; 688,455 bytes, 49 PDF pages; printed page = PDF page. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY — an official curriculum programme: it states what is required to be taught and teaches nothing itself.

**Book 2:** *Programa de matemàtiques de segona ensenyança* (curriculum programme, Grades 7–10) — Andorra, Govern d'Andorra, Escola Andorrana; Decret 355/2023 (BOPA Núm. 91, 19-7-2023); ISBN 978-99920-0-847-2; 1,808,872 bytes, 56 PDF pages; printed page = PDF page (the BOPA folio). TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY — an official curriculum programme: it states what is required to be taught and teaches nothing itself.

**Book 4:** *Programa de matemàtiques cientificotècniques del batxillerat general* (curriculum programme, Grades 11–12) — Andorra, Govern d'Andorra, Escola Andorrana; Decret 242/2025 (BOPA Núm. 83, 9-7-2025); ISBN 978-99920-82-23-2; 4,325,201 bytes, 38 PDF pages; printed page = PDF page (the BOPA folio). TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY — an official curriculum programme: it states what is required to be taught and teaches nothing itself.

## Angola added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Angola's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **6 books, all pupil textbooks: Matemática, Manuais da 1.ª–6.ª Classe (Ministério da Educação — MED, Angola; updated editions of 2021, in Portuguese). There is no teacher guide in the Angola Maths set in hand, so every Angola row in this course is a pupil-textbook row — there are no teacher-evidence rows, and Angola counts in every lesson it stocks** — and placed 229 teaching items. The expansion map's “6 student + 0 teacher = 6” is correct: download-lists/angola.md, the phase3-staging checklist, textbooks.tsv, phase3-push-state.json (“pushed”) and the staging books.json files all give the same six distinct pupil textbooks, and nothing else. None of the six PDFs was on local disk (pusher-secured only), so all six were fetched fresh for the read from the books' recorded second official source, the ministry's Xilonga host, byte-exact against the records. One set nuance: the portal's 32-manual set contains no 2.ª Classe Matemática at all — the 2.ª book in hand (Book 2) exists only as an unlinked Xilonga file (MAT02-LR.pdf) found by pattern probe on 2026-10-23. A Guia Prático Matemática 6ª exists only as an exact-link reader copy and is NOT in hand — cited nowhere; if one is ever secured it enters as teacher evidence only. The Book 2 and Book 4 PDFs carry a massively duplicated text layer (the same text overprinted many times per page) — a production flaw of the files; the content is fully legible and both books were read in full from it. Twelve book errors are recorded in the placements; they are carried in the book entries below and taught corrected. The placements' named exclusions — the apresentação (ministerial foreword letter) and anthem/flag/civic front matter, the bibliografia pages, pure colouring, tracing and copying practice with no new concept, the editorial production notes printed inside the books, and exercise sets as such (exercises practise the item under which they sit) — are stocked nowhere. Printed-page bases: Books 1 and 3 — printed page = PDF page − 1; Books 2, 4, 5 and 6 — printed page = PDF page. Into this lesson the placements stock 11 items (all from pupil textbooks) from Book 1, Book 2, Book 3 and Book 4.

**Book 1:** *Matemática — Manual da 1.ª Classe* (pupil textbook, Grade 1) — Angola, Ministério da Educação (MED), Editora Moderna, 2021, 1.ª Edição, ISBN 978-989-762-267-0; 4,293,873 bytes, 81 PDF pp. Pages cited are the book's printed pages (printed page = PDF page − 1).

**Book 2:** *Matemática — Manual da 2.ª Classe* (pupil textbook, Grade 2) — Angola, Ministério da Educação (MED), Mayamba Editora, 2021, 1.ª Edição, ISBN 978-989-761-297-8; 3,437,647 bytes, 56 PDF pp. Pages cited are the book's printed pages (printed page = PDF page). Book error, taught corrected: printed p. 23 calls the minuend “diminuendo ou aditivo” — “aditivo” is wrong for subtraction; the course teaches minuend. Editorial production notes are printed as lesson content on printed p. 50 — production matter, cited nowhere. The PDF's text layer is massively duplicated (the same text overprinted many times per page) — a production flaw of the file; the content is fully legible and the book was read in full from it.

**Book 3:** *Matemática — Manual da 3.ª Classe* (pupil textbook, Grade 3) — Angola, Ministério da Educação (MED), Progresso Editora, 2021, 1.ª Edição, ISBN 978-989-762-270-0; 1,798,728 bytes, 85 PDF pp. Pages cited are the book's printed pages (printed page = PDF page − 1). Book errors, taught corrected: printed p. 21 repeats the minuend misnomer “diminuendo ou aditivo” (teach: minuend); printed p. 36's decimal-multiplication worked example is internally inconsistent — it states 4,15 × 2,3, lays out 3,15 and prints 7,245 (correct: 4,15 × 2,3 = 9,545); the exercise under “Noção de circunferência” at printed p. 44 repeats the parallel-lines exercise verbatim (a copy-paste error in the book); printed p. 62 prints the false line “20 m = 200 cm” (correct: 20 m = 2 000 cm); printed p. 64's formula under decâmetro repeats “1 hm = 100 m” (correct: 1 dam = 10 m).

**Book 4:** *Matemática — Manual da 4.ª Classe* (pupil textbook, Grade 4) — Angola, Ministério da Educação (MED), Mayamba Editora, 2021, 1.ª Edição, ISBN 978-989-761-296-1; 1,983,954 bytes, 112 PDF pp. Pages cited are the book's printed pages (printed page = PDF page). Book error, taught corrected: printed p. 43 claims division “is only possible if the dividend is a multiple of the divisor” (exact division only) — contradicted by Book 3's own division-with-remainder teaching; the course teaches division with remainder. Editorial production notes are printed as page content on printed pp. 17, 18, 21, 24, 25, 28, 30 and 45 — production matter, cited nowhere; the Tema 3 heading is misprinted inside the book (at printed p. 102); PDF pp. 106–112 are blank/back matter apart from the bibliografia (printed p. 107), cited nowhere. The PDF's text layer is massively duplicated (the same text overprinted many times per page) — a production flaw of the file; the content is fully legible and the book was read in full from it.

## Bahamas added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read the Bahamas' whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **4 documents, all of them Bahamas Ministry of Education curriculum or examination-syllabus documents; 0 pupil (student) textbooks and 0 teacher guides** — and placed 496 requirement items. A curriculum states what is REQUIRED to be taught; it teaches nothing itself. Every Bahamas item in this section is therefore CURRICULUM EVIDENCE — a statement of what the Bahamas requires, worded as a requirement — cited as the evidence kind it is (Book 3's items are examination-SYLLABUS evidence, on the same standing); the Bahamas is counted in NO lesson's taught-in figure, on the standing Ghana/Barbados evidence-only precedent, until a Bahamas pupil book is in hand and read. Set correction, as the placements prove it: the expansion map recorded the in-hand set as “4 student + 0 teacher” — the count of 4 is right, the kind is wrong; all four documents are ministry requirement documents (kind = curriculum in both the staging books.json files and textbooks.tsv). The file the records title *BJC Mathematics Curriculum, Grades 7–9* is actually the *Bahamas Junior Certificate 044 Mathematics Syllabus 2003* — a 10-page scanned examination syllabus with no text layer, read visually page by page — and the held Senior High file is the July 2019 revision of the Grades 10–12 guidelines; both are placed as what they are. No pupil textbook or teacher guide is in hand: the pupil books Bahamas schools use are commercial (Hodder *Bahamas Primary Mathematics* Books 1–6 — existence verified in the records, no file held; Books 1–2 name Silver Burdett Ginn Mathematics and Harcourt Math in classroom use, and Book 4's bibliography names STP Caribbean Mathematics, Mathematics: A Complete Course, Fundamental Mathematics for the Caribbean and Nelson Caribbean Mathematics — named only, none held), and the one school-compiled pupil text with an exact link in the records (Akhepran International Academy, *Mathematics BJC Textbook — Grade 7*, 2023) was never downloaded and sits in no record set, so it is not part of this read and is cited nowhere. The placements' counting grain is one item per distinct numbered objective in each guidelines book's Scope of Work body — the Content column read with every objective — Book 3 at one item per numbered topic row of its Topics table and Book 4 at one item per CONCEPTS/TOPICS block; an objective restated in full on a continuation page is counted once, at its first full statement, and the two requirements the documents restate inside a second strand (Book 1 Grade 2 estimation, item 69; Book 2 Grade 5 rounding of decimals, item 256) are stocked as the placements place them, each row naming the restatement. The placements' named exclusions — Benchmarks, Scope-and-Sequence charts, pacing guides and Essential Questions in Books 1–2; the Problems of the Day sets, Materials Lists, assessment rubrics, the Children's Literature list and References (record items R1–R7 and R9–R11); Book 3's examination administration, its 19 general aims and its erratum sheet (R8); Book 4's Scope and Sequence charts, Glossary and Technology Integration pages — are stocked nowhere. Book errors found in the read are carried corrected on the rows they touch. Into this lesson the placements stock 18 requirement-evidence items from Book 1 and Book 2.

**Book 1:** *Primary School Mathematics Curriculum Guidelines, Grades 1–3* (curriculum guidelines) — Bahamas, Ministry of Education, Department of Education, September 2010; 5,558,181 bytes, 263 PDF pages; Scope of Work printed page = PDF page − 51. CURRICULUM EVIDENCE ONLY — a curriculum states what is required to be taught; it teaches nothing itself.

CURRICULUM EVIDENCE ONLY — a curriculum states what is required to be taught; it teaches nothing itself.

The Bahamas' class for Maths is PARTIAL (evidence-only): all 4 in-hand documents are read and stocked, but no pupil textbook is in hand; it advances only when a pupil book file — a Hodder *Bahamas Primary Mathematics* volume, or the Akhepran Grade 7 BJC textbook whose exact link already sits in the download list — lands in hand and is read.

## Belize added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Belize's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **3 documents, all of them Belize Ministry of Education *Mathematics Curriculum: Teacher's Guide* books (August 2012) — Infant 1, Standard 1 and Standard IV; 0 pupil (student) textbooks** — and placed 108 teaching items. Every document in the set is a teacher's guide: it states the learning outcomes pupils must reach and teaches the teacher how to teach each one (comments, sample lesson objectives, specific content, sample activities and assessment). Every Belize item in this section is therefore TEACHER-GUIDE EVIDENCE — Belize's guides teach teachers how to teach the topic; no Belize pupil textbook is in hand — cited as the evidence kind it is; Belize is counted in NO lesson's taught-in figure, on the standing Ghana evidence-only precedent, until a Belize pupil book is in hand and read. No pupil Maths book is in hand: the pupil books on the official National Textbook Program list (*My BZ Math* Infant 1–Standard 4, Macmillan; *Caribbean Primary Math* Bk 6 for Standard 5, Hodder; *Active Math* for Standard 6, Macmillan) are all recorded missing in the library files, and the guides' own textbook linkages point to the legacy pupil series *Primary Mathematics for Belize* (Hodder, by term codes Y1T1…/S1T1…), which is named by the guides only — not in hand, not read, and not a course source. Three further Maths documents — the *Primary curriculum: Mathematics* division webs (Lower Division, Middle Division, Upper Division) — are recorded at exact link only, never downloaded and in no books.json; they are not part of this read and are cited nowhere. The placements' counting grain is one item per distinct numbered learning outcome in each guide's Standards and Learning Outcomes section (35 outcomes in each guide, 105 in all) plus 3 items taught in a detailed section but stated in no outcome of that guide (items 64, 65 and 87, marked D); outcome numbering is continuous across the series (Infant 1 carries M1.1–M20.1, Standard 1 continues at M1.10, Standard IV at M1.16), which is why the codes do not restart at 1 in the later guides. Printed page = PDF page in all three guides. The placements' named exclusions — the guides' front matter (programme philosophy: goals, rationale, assessment discussion), strand introductions and Big Ideas prose, sample lesson objectives, sample assessments, sample activities and print/technology resource columns as apparatus, the shared manipulatives list, the websites lists, the shared Mathematics Glossary (carried in the placements as record item R2 — a teacher reference list, cited nowhere as content), and the standards each guide marks N/A at its level (Book 1: M2 place value and M13 equations; Book 2: M14 measurement concepts; Book 3: M5 position/ranking numbers and M14) — are stocked nowhere. The 14 book errors and inconsistencies found in the read (placements E1–E14) are carried corrected on the rows they touch: item 35 is placed as data interpretation, not probability, though the guide files it under its probability standard (E5); item 51's set language is taught with “complement” for the book's “compliment” (E6); item 66's many-to-one correspondence is taught as one symbol or unit standing for many objects, not the guide's function-style definition (E8); item 82 is stocked WITHOUT the outcome's fourth bullet (“expand powers with same base to add or subtract”), which is not a valid add/subtract procedure (E9); item 81 is placed as stated and flagged as vague and advanced for its level (E10); item 104 is placed as measurement and taught with the standard measurement meanings, the guide's own definition of precision being a data-management sense (E13); and item 106 follows the guide's own correct definitions — its assessment text's “medium” for median is an error (E12). Into this lesson the placements stock 14 teacher-guide-evidence items from Book 1, Book 2 and Book 3.

**Book 1:** *Mathematics Curriculum: Teacher's Guide, Infant 1* (teacher guide, Grade 1) — Belize, Ministry of Education, August 2012; 738,164 bytes, 58 PDF pages. Standards and Learning Outcomes section printed pp. 12–16; detailed teaching sections printed pp. 17–52. Printed page = PDF page. TEACHER-GUIDE EVIDENCE ONLY — the guide teaches the teacher how to teach the topic; it is not a pupil textbook.

**Book 2:** *Mathematics Curriculum: Teacher's Guide, Standard 1* (teacher guide, Grade 3) — Belize, Ministry of Education, August 2012; 803,827 bytes, 62 PDF pages. Standards and Learning Outcomes section printed pp. 12–16; detailed teaching sections printed pp. 17–56. Printed page = PDF page. Outcome numbering continues the series from Book 1 (M1.10 onwards). TEACHER-GUIDE EVIDENCE ONLY — the guide teaches the teacher how to teach the topic; it is not a pupil textbook.

## Brazil added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Brazil's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDF — **1 book, a pupil textbook: *Matemática — Livro Didático Público — Ensino Médio* (SEED-PR), the Paraná state public textbook for Ensino Médio (Grades 10–12), in Portuguese. There is no teacher guide in the Brazil Maths set in hand, so every Brazil row in this course is a pupil-textbook row — there are no teacher-evidence rows, and Brazil counts in every lesson it stocks** — and placed 128 teaching items. The expansion map's “1 student + 0 teacher = 1” is correct: books.json (grade-10/gov/brazil) and textbooks.tsv each carry exactly one Brazil Mathematics entry, and phase3-push-state.json lists the file as pushed. The staging folder for the book is empty, so there was no local copy to read, and the authenticated GitHub asset route failed in this pass (the mid-size-books release's asset listing repeatedly disconnected through the proxy, and the asset's download URL answered 404) — the GitHub copy's existence and size were therefore NOT verified here, and a library-side check of that asset is owed (a route failure, not a negative finding). The book was instead read from its own recorded source_url — the Wayback capture banked by the 2026-10-22 CDX prep (capture 20240421222141) — which landed at exactly the prep's expected 6,379,928 B, with 240 pages and the correct cover and catalogue block; the book read is certainly this book. Printed page = PDF page throughout. Four book errors found in the read (placements E1–E4) are carried corrected on the rows they touch — in the sequences-and-series (E1), ratio-and-proportion (E2), coordinate-geometry (E3), barycentres (E4) and loci (E4) lessons; two further misprints are recorded, not taught (R5: the risk exponent typeset once as 0.2525 for 0.2528, printed p. 58 — noted on the item 28 row in the logarithms-and-exponentials lesson; R6: glucose printed “C6A12O6”, printed p. 35, a chemistry misprint), and the Aristarchus bound passage (printed p. 169, record item R7) could not be confirmed either way as extracted and is cited nowhere. The placements' named exclusions — the four section introductions and the general presentation (framing and history), the context essays and debates that carry no new maths, the biographical and historical passages except where the passage itself teaches a method, the “Obras Consultadas” reference lists after every chapter, and the blank “Anotações” pages — are stocked nowhere. Into this lesson the placements stock 1 item (all from the pupil textbook) from Book 1.

**Book 1:** *Matemática — Livro Didático Público — Ensino Médio* (pupil textbook, Ensino Médio — Grades 10–12) — Brazil, Secretaria de Estado da Educação do Paraná (SEED-PR), Curitiba, 2nd edition 2006, ISBN 85-85380-39-X; 6,379,928 bytes, 240 PDF pp., MD5 e3dac71a6d075dd142282376da6fa785. Two set corrections are proved by the read and carried exactly. (1) The staged record files this book as the mainstream Azerbaijani-sector Grade 2 textbook; it is not — it is the special-schools simplified-programme edition, **Part 2 only**, covering numbers 13–20; simplified Part 1 (76 pp) was never staged and is not in hand, and the mainstream Grade 2 *Riyaziyyat* (portal ids 664/665; older whole-book uploads of 147/147/162 pp, all checked and ruled out by page count) is not in hand at all. ## Bhutan added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Bhutan's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **16 files: 13 student books and 3 teacher's guides of the Royal Government of Bhutan's *Understanding Mathematics* series (Department of School Education / Ministry of Education and Skills Development), English-medium throughout** — and placed 657 pupil teaching items and 30 teacher-evidence items. **Set correction, proved by the read: the expansion map's count of 10 student + 3 teacher is WRONG — the map counted only the textbooks.tsv snapshot (PP, I, II, IV–X) and missed the Class III, XI and XII student books recorded as secured and pushed in the staging books.json files and the push state; the true in-hand set is 13 student + 3 teacher = 16 files, every one grabbed and verified (byte size on every file; MD5 against books.json on the 11 files that carry one — all match; page counts match every record) and read in full.** The Class I staging-path collision is resolved: the main-branch grade-01 file is the student activity book (Book 3); the Class I teacher's guide (Book 4) is a separate, MD5-verified file that books.json records under the same staging path name. **Critical filing flag, carried exactly: both BHSEC filings claim "complete book, not a sample/preview" — FALSE. The Class XI file holds only chapters 1–5 of the 26 its own contents lists and the Class XII file only chapters 1–4 of the 19 its contents lists; only the chapters actually present were placed, and both books are cited as the partial excerpts they are.** The 3 teacher's guides are teacher evidence only: their items are named below as teacher evidence and are never counted as pupil teaching. The PP activity book's Reproducible Pages (printed pp. 89–116, cut-out masters) and Summative Assessment Recording Sheets (pp. 117–132) are apparatus and are stocked nowhere; its assessment sheets renumber two chapters against the body (Data Management is Ch. 6 there, Ch. 9 in the body; Ordinal Numbers and Halves Ch. 11 there, Ch. 10 in the body) — body numbering is used throughout. The Class 1 activity book's Reproducible Pages (printed pp. 75–80) and Summative Assessment Recording Sheets (pp. 81–100) are apparatus and are stocked nowhere. The Class 2 activity book's Summative Assessment Recording Sheets (printed pp. 99–118) are apparatus and are stocked nowhere. The pupil Chapter 9 pages open at Lesson 3 (Interpreting Bar Graphs) — no pupil Lessons 1–2 for that chapter appear in the file; the guide's parallel survey and data-collection lessons stand as teacher evidence instead. Book errors in the Class 2 book are carried corrected: its imprint prints the copyright year as "20234", read as 2023 as on its guide, and its Chapter 6 Lesson 6 tells pupils to draw a cylinder and label its base and apex — a cylinder has no apex; the apex belongs to the cone in the same lesson. The Class 3 textbook's Chapter Overviews, Chapter Reviews and course-objectives/assessment front matter are apparatus and are stocked nowhere. The Class V textbook's Getting Started spreads, GAME and CONNECTIONS features, Unit Revisions, Glossary, Measurement Reference and Answers are apparatus/practice and are stocked nowhere. The Class VI textbook's Getting Started spreads, GAME and CONNECTIONS features, Unit Revisions, Glossary, Measurement Reference and Answers are apparatus/practice and are stocked nowhere. Its contents numbers two different sections 6.2.2 ("Bisectors" and "EXPLORE: Sorting Quadrilaterals"); the second is cited with the duplication named. ## Cabo Verde added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Cabo Verde’s whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **3 books, all of them student (pupil) manuals of the Ministério da Educação de Cabo Verde: *Manual de Matemática 3º ano* (Edição 2019), *Manual de Matemática 5º ano* (Edição 2020) and *Matemática 7º ano — Manual do(a) aluno(a)* (Edição 2020); 0 teacher guides** — and placed 197 teaching items (Grade 3: 47, Grade 5: 69, Grade 7: 81; 391 PDF pages, every page read). The expansion map’s count of 3 student + 0 teacher is CORRECT. Set boundary, proved from the records: `textbooks.tsv` carries exactly 17 Cabo Verde rows (all Government, grades 1–7), of which exactly three are Mathematics, all student; the download list’s §C enumerates 93 ministry manuals, including Maths manuals for grades 1, 2, 4, 6, 8, 9, 10 and 11 and applied-Maths manuals — every one of those is exact-link-only (no TSV row, no staging file, no GitHub copy) and NOT in hand, so none is cited here; counting any of them would be a set error. No duplicates exist in the set: three distinct grades, three distinct books. Printed page = PDF page in all three books. The placements’ named exclusions — front matter, unit openers, objectives lists and the closing national-anthem page in the Grade 5 and Grade 7 books; the Grade 3 book’s cover plate and welcome/mascot pages (PDF pp. 1, 3, 5), its part-opener pages (PDF pp. 55, 85, 107, 111), its closing test “Teste” (printed pp. 125–129 — mixed assessment re-using taught content, no new teaching) and the flag / national-anthem page (PDF p. 130) — and pure exercise / consolidation blocks that teach nothing new are stocked nowhere. The 12 book errors found in the read (2 in the Grade 5 book, 5 in the Grade 7 book, 5 in the Grade 3 book) are carried corrected on the rows they touch.
